ids\n        # with ``parameterize`` produce one expanded EvalSpec per row.\n        expanded: list[EvalSpec] = []\n        for entry in raw_evals:\n            expanded.extend(self._expand_entry(entry))\n\n        return ParsedSuite(\n            name=manifest[\"name\"],\n            version=manifest[\"version\"],\n            description=manifest[\"description\"],\n            defaults=manifest.get(\"defaults\", {}),\n            required_binaries=tuple(manifest.get(\"required_binaries\", ())),\n            optional_capabilities=tuple(manifest.get(\"optional_capabilities\", ())),\n            evals=tuple(expanded),\n            source_path=manifest_path,\n        )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # internals\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _check_capabilities(self, entry: Mapping[str, Any]) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Funnel one eval's ``requires`` clause through the resolver.\"\"\"\n\n        required = tuple(entry.get(\"requires\", ()))\n        missing = tuple(self.capability_resolver.resolve(entry[\"id\"], required))\n        if missing:\n            raise UnresolvedCapability(eval_id=entry[\"id\"], missing=missing)\n\n    def _expand_entry(self, entry: Mapping[str, Any]) -> list[EvalSpec]:\n        \"\"\"Materialise ``parameterize`` rows into one ``EvalSpec`` per row.\n\n        Expansion convention (design-spec §5):\n\n        * Base id with no ``parameterize`` → one ``EvalSpec`` with the\n          static id unchanged.\n        * Base id with N ``parameterize`` rows → N ``EvalSpec`` rows\n          whose ids are ``{base}.{1..N}``.\n\n        Each expanded id is re-checked against :func:`validate_eval_id`\n        before it returns — this is the load-bearing FR-SCH2 application\n        that the security model relies on. The current 1-based index\n        convention is safe by construction (digits-only suffix), but\n        the guard is mandatory because future expansion strategies\n        (e.g., named indices) could leak unsafe characters.\n\n        Substitution of ``{{key}}`` tokens inside other fields is the\n        runtime layer's responsibility, not the loader's: the schema\n        already constrained per-row keys to ``^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$``,\n        so any token a row carries is safe to interpolate later. The\n        loader's job is to preserve the row data on the expanded\n        ``EvalSpec`` (currently round-tripped via ``EvalSpec.from_dict``\n        keeping ``parameterize`` populated) so the runner can apply\n        substitutions per-id.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        parameterize_rows = entry.get(\"parameterize\", ())\n        if not parameterize_rows:\n            return [EvalSpec.from_dict(entry)]\n\n        expanded: list[EvalSpec] = []\n        base_id = entry[\"id\"]\n        for index, _row in enumerate(parameterize_rows, start=1):\n            expanded_id = f\"{base_id}.{index}\"\n            # FR-SCH2 re-check — see method docstring for rationale.\n            validate_eval_id(expanded_id)\n            expanded_entry = dict(entry)\n            expanded_entry[\"id\"] = expanded_id\n            expanded.append(EvalSpec.from_dict(expanded_entry))\n        return expanded\n","numLines":85,"startLine":540,"totalLines":624}}}

The function accepts\n17\t  any value at runtime so non-string scalars (`int`, `bool`, `None`,\n18\t  `bytes`) that may leak through YAML decoding are rejected via the\n19\t  type guard rather than slipping past the regex check.\n20\t- **Output:** `None` on success (the function is a pure guard).\n21\t- **Error behaviour:** raises `InvalidEvalId` carrying the offending\n22\t  value on `InvalidEvalId.eval_id`. CLI callers MUST map this to\n23\t  `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n24\t\n25\t## The regex\n26\t\n27\t```python\n28\tEVAL_ID_REGEX = re.compile(r\"^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$\")\n29\t```\n30\t\n31\tThis is the single source of truth for the runtime guard. The same\n32\tregex literal is encoded into `suites/suite.schema.json` (T01.02) as\n33\tthe `evalIdString` `pattern`, so schema validation rejects unsafe\n34\t**static** ids at the schema layer too; `validate_eval_id` is the\n35\tauthoritative runtime check the security model relies on (especially\n36\tfor post-parameterize-expanded ids that never touch the schema).\n37\t\n38\tThe pattern accepts:\n39\t\n40\t| Shape           | Examples                          |\n41\t|-----------------|-----------------------------------|\n42\t| Canonical eval  | `E1`, `E2`, `E15`, `D15`          |\n43\t| Parameter-ized  | `E2.1`, `E2.2`, `E2.10`, `Foo42.7`|\n44\t| Single letter   | `A`, `Z9`                         |\n45\t| Mixed-case body | `Test1`, `ABC123`                 |\n46\t\n47\tIt rejects everything else; the named negative cases below are the\n48\tfloor, not the ceiling.\n49\t\n50\t## Negative-case checklist (FR-SCH2 / NFR-SEC1)\n51\t\n52\t| Category                  | Examples                                                 | Why it must fail                                              |\n53\t|---------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------|\n54\t| Empty string              | `\"\"`                                                     | AC bullet; trivially escapes `home_root / \"\" / \"home\"`        |\n55\t| Path-traversal prefix     | `../home`, `../../etc`, `..`                             | AC bullet; escapes the scratch root via `Path` join semantics |\n56\t| Absolute path             | `/etc`, `/tmp/eval-runs`                                 | AC bullet; `Path(\"/abs\") / \"home\"` discards `home_root`       |\n57\t| Embedded separator        | `foo/bar`, `foo\\\\bar`, `E1/x`, `./foo`, `.`              | Lets attacker carve sub-directories under `home_root`         |\n58\t| Leading-digit id          | `1E`, `9`, `0E`, `1`, `12.3`                             | AC bullet; {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Revh4te7kNjsvFLoyRi1rH","type":"tool_result","content":"# D-0005 — implementation notes\n\n**Task:** T01.05 — Implement FR-SCH2 eval-id regex guard\n**Date:** 2026-05-20\n\n## Design decisions\n\n1. **Constants over magic literals.** `EVAL_ID_REGEX` is compiled once at\n   import; `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE` is its own constant rather than\n   reusing `SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`. Same numeric value (2), different\n   semantic intent. Lets call sites branch on the failure class without\n   coupling the two via a shared constant.\n\n2. **Type guard precedes regex check.** YAML can produce non-string\n   scalars (int, bool, None) for any node — even ones the schema\n   declares as `string` if the schema check is bypassed (post-expansion\n   code path constructs the id programmatically). The type guard raises\n   `InvalidEvalId` directly so non-str inputs never reach the\n   `EVAL_ID_REGEX.fullmatch` call.\n\n3. **`fullmatch`, not `match`.** Belt-and-braces: the regex is already\n   anchored (`^...$`) but `fullmatch` ignores `re.MULTILINE` quirks and\n   makes the contract intent obvious to readers.\n\n4. **No length cap.** The regex itself is a polynomial-time match on a\n   bounded character class so ReDoS is not a concern; a length cap\n   would be a separate, orthogonal AC and is not in this task's scope.\n\n5. **`InvalidEvalId.eval_id` preserves original value.** Reporter\n   output uses `repr()` rendering so whitespace, empty strings, and\n   control characters survive forensically. The attribute is `Any`\n   typed because non-str inputs are also valid inhabitants.\n\n## Behaviour delta vs schema-layer enforcement\n\nThe same regex is encoded as the `evalIdString` `pattern` in\n`suite.schema.json` (T01.02). For well-formed manifests the two layers\nagree by construction; the runtime guard exists to:\n\n- Catch post-parameterize-expanded ids (which never traverse the\n  schema layer).\n- Catch programmatic construction inside the loader / runner (e.g., a\n  future feature that derives `eval_id` from a hash or counter).\n- Provide a single typed exception (`InvalidEvalId`) that the CLI maps\n  to exit code 2 without round-tripping through `jsonschema`.\n\n## Why two test files\n\n- `tests/cli/eval/test_eval_id_regex.py` (this task, T01.05) — owns\n  the unit contract of the function: positive cases, negative cases,\n  type guard, error surface, exit-code mapping.\n- `tests/cli/eval/test_path_traversal.py` (T01.08 / NFR-SEC1) — owns\n  the security-focused negative case set as a separate first-class\n  deliverable that cross-links to TEST-001 (T01.23).\n\nSome overlap is intentional: the unit file demonstrates that the\nfunction rejects the named AC cases; T01.08's file is the dedicated\nsecurity gate that ties the same cases to FR-SCH2 + TEST-001\ntraceability.\n\n## Sub-agent verification\n\nTier=STRICT with Risk=High and \"security\" risk driver. Same numeric value (2), different\n   semantic intent. Lets call sites branch on the failure class without\n   coupling the two via a shared constant.\n\n2. **Type guard precedes regex check.** YAML can produce non-string\n   scalars (int, bool, None) for any node — even ones the schema\n   declares as `string` if the schema check is bypassed (post-expansion\n   code path constructs the id programmatically). The type guard raises\n   `InvalidEvalId` directly so non-str inputs never reach the\n   `EVAL_ID_REGEX.fullmatch` call.\n\n3. **`fullmatch`, not `match`.** Belt-and-braces: the regex is already\n   anchored (`^...$`) but `fullmatch` ignores `re.MULTILINE` quirks and\n   makes the contract intent obvious to readers.\n\n4. **No length cap.** The regex itself is a polynomial-time match on a\n   bounded character class so ReDoS is not a concern; a length cap\n   would be a separate, orthogonal AC and is not in this task's scope.\n\n5. **`InvalidEvalId.eval_id` preserves original value.** Reporter\n   output uses `repr()` rendering so whitespace, empty strings, and\n   control characters survive forensically. The attribute is `Any`\n   typed because non-str inputs are also valid inhabitants.\n\n## Behaviour delta vs schema-layer enforcement\n\nThe same regex is encoded as the `evalIdString` `pattern` in\n`suite.schema.json` (T01.02). The\n    guard MUST reject before that join is constructed.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    with pytest.raises(InvalidEvalId) as excinfo:\n        validate_eval_id(\"/etc\")\n    assert excinfo.value.eval_id == \"/etc\"\n\n\ndef test_rejects_bare_dotdot() -> None:\n    \"\"\"AC bullet 3: ``..`` on its own must be rejected.\n\n    Bare ``..`` collapses to the parent of the scratch root under\n    ``Path`` join semantics, escaping containment by one directory.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    with pytest.raises(InvalidEvalId) as excinfo:\n        validate_eval_id(\"..\")\n    assert excinfo.value.eval_id == \"..\"\n\n\ndef test_rejects_empty_string() -> None:\n    \"\"\"AC bullet 4: the empty string must be rejected.\n\n    ``home_root / \"\" / \"home\"`` is a no-op and would collide with any\n    eval that legitimately needed a ``home`` subdirectory under the\n    scratch root. A pure unit-level call to\n`validate_eval_id(\"E2.../../etc/passwd\")` would only prove the regex\nfunction rejects — not that the loader *invokes* the function at the\nexpansion site.\n\n## {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01M2AkQTSXjqKCCTrBmkjDkM","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01W9Z3VKoBkdwYxJt5zUBvTh","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0007/notes.md","content":"# D-0007 — implementation notes\n\n**Task:** T01.08 — Author NFR-SEC1 path-traversal prevention test set\n**Date:** 2026-05-20\n\n## Design decisions\n\n1. **One test per AC bullet, not bundled `@pytest.mark.parametrize`.**\n   The 7 named NFR-SEC1 categories each get a dedicated `def\n   test_rejects_*` so the failure name a reviewer sees (\"`empty_string`\n   regression\") maps 1:1 to the AC checklist. Sub-cases inside a\n   single category (e.g. multiple leading-digit shapes) are\n   parametrize-collapsed because the *category* is the auditable unit.\n\n2. **Integration-level parameterized-unsafe test.** The post-expansion\n   guard is the load-bearing FR-SCH2 application. Calling\n   `validate_eval_id` on an unsafe expanded string in isolation would\n   only test the regex; using `SuiteLoader.load()` with a hostile\n   `_expand_entry` patch proves the loader actually invokes the\n   re-check after expansion. This is the security guarantee the spec\n   relies on.\n\n3. **No regex sanity test here.** That belongs to `test_eval_id_regex.py`\n   (T01.05) — `EVAL_ID_REGEX.pattern` is asserted there as a single\n   source of truth. Duplicating it here would risk silent drift if a\n   future patch updates the regex but the dedicated security checklist\n   wasn't re-read.\n\n4. **Defence-in-depth FS-write assertion.** `validate_eval_id` is pure\n   and synchronous, but the test\n   `test_no_fs_write_when_traversal_id_rejected` snapshots a sandbox\n   `tmp_path` before/after a rejection.
